**Trade-off accepted:** Opening sixty-four photographs cost a working session, and the tags are still one person's judgement about her own clothes rather than ground truth — 130's limit stands, and a validator proves a row is legal and never that it is true. What changed is the class of error that survives: guesses from a word are gone, and what remains is judgement about an image, which is the same standard 1.11's golden set will be held to.

**Two subcategories that looked populated were populated by wrong filenames.** `top_shirt_black` is a ribbed jersey V-neck with no buttons and no collar — a `t_shirt` — and `outerwear_coat_black` is a cropped shearling aviator — a `jacket`. Opening them emptied **`shirt`** and reduced **`coat`** to one, which is why 138's recount moved as far as it did. `tote` and `clutch` had emptied the same way one pass earlier. A subcategory count taken from filenames overstated the wardrobe's coverage in four places.

**Four vocabulary gaps, all found by looking at photographs before 1.11 ran, none found by 1.11.** `turquoise` on a satin cami, which the seventeen-value `ColorPrimary` cannot name and which ships as the nearest legal `blue` (**139**). **Knee-high** on a pair of harness riding boots: `Length` gives shoes `ankle` and nothing above it, so `full` ships as the least-wrong word. And **the bag vocabulary has no word for four of six bags** — `SUBCATEGORIES[bag]` is `tote · crossbody · shoulder · clutch · backpack`, and four of these are flap or fold-over bags on chain straps that are `shoulder` only by elimination, while the wardrobe contains no tote and no clutch at all. That is three gaps on three different axes from one folder of one person's clothes — a fourth follows below and is deliberately kept apart from them — and the thing these three have in common is that **a person looking at a photograph found each of them, before any accuracy run existed.** 1.11 should carry them in as known cases rather than rediscover them: for each, the interesting question is not whether the vocabulary is short a word — it is — but what the *model* returns when shown the same garment, since a nearest-legal answer, a different nearest-legal answer and an out-of-vocabulary word coerced to null are three different failures that look identical in a column of nulls.

**The fourth is `suede`, and it is a different kind of gap from the other three — which is the point of listing it separately rather than beside them.** `Material` has nine values and none is `suede`, so three rows ship as `leather`: the brown shearling jacket, the black ankle boots and the grey shoulder bag. **`leather` is not wrong there. Suede *is* leather** — napped leather — so the tag is true and merely lossy, where `blue` on a turquoise cami is simply false and `full` on a knee-high boot is a word doing a second job. That distinction decides what 1.11 does with each: a model answering `leather` for suede is **correct and should score as correct**, while a model answering `blue` for turquoise is wrong and would score as right against this seed's own tags. A gap that costs detail and a gap that costs truth must not be counted together, and they would have been if this one had simply been appended to the list.

**In all three suede rows the `display_name` carries the word the tag column cannot** — "brown suede shearling jacket", "black suede ankle boots", "grey suede chain shoulder bag". That is not decoration: `display_name` is the free-text field 1.11 already plans to mine for vocabulary words the schema discarded, and this is the first case where the word being mined was put there deliberately by a person rather than surviving accidentally from model output. It is a worked example of why that plan is worth carrying out.

**`water_resistant` is `false` on all sixty-four rows, and that is a decision rather than an unfilled column.** Nothing in this wardrobe is rainproof. The two shearling jackets and the wool coat are the only plausible candidates and they handle drizzle rather than rain; their protection against weather is already carried by `warmth: 5`, and the column is a boolean with no room for "fine in light rain". Written down because an all-`false` column reads as a field nobody filled in, and this one was filled in sixty-four times with the same answer. **The consequence belongs to Stage 4:** the packing feature reads this column, and it must treat `false` as *not rainproof* rather than as *no information* — on this data those two readings give different packing lists, and only the first one is true. `02-DATA-MODEL.md` already types the column `NOT NULL DEFAULT FALSE`, so there is no third state to fall back on and none is wanted.

**The seed no longer covers HEIC end to end, and no document ever claimed it did.** `samples/` held one HEIC — the brown shearling aviator — and it was replaced mid-task with a correctly named JPEG of the same garment, so **there is now no HEIC anywhere in the seed set.** Grepped before writing this: every HEIC mention in `docs/` is task 1.1's `f_auto` experiment, the accepted-format list in `04-API-SPEC.md`, `01-ARCHITECTURE.md` and `05-FRONTEND-SPEC.md`, or `03-AI-CONTRACTS.md`'s note that the one live schema call used a HEIC. **None of them says the seed exercises the format**, so nothing needed correcting — the claim that it would was made in conversation and never reached a file. What is true and worth having written down: `tests/unit/test_storage.py` covers HEIC and HEIF **signature detection** with synthetic twelve-byte headers, so the magic-byte branch is defended, and nothing anywhere runs a real HEIC through `upload_image` → Cloudinary → `build_url` → delivery. `AUDITS.md` **O-14** already owns that gap and already states it correctly; it is extended there to record that the seed was briefly a candidate to close it and is not one.

## 142 — The weather rule is built from `temp_max_c`, decided by the example that contradicted itself

**Decision:** `GET /weather` and every later caller feed **`temp_max_c`** to `build_rule`. The minimum is returned in the response and used by nothing.
**Alternative:** `temp_min_c`; the mean of the two; or an hourly reading at a fixed hour.
**Reasoning:** `03-AI-CONTRACTS.md` gives `build_rule(temp_c, …)` one temperature and `04-API-SPEC.md` returns two, and no document said which. What settled it is that `04`'s own worked example is only self-consistent under the maximum: it returns `temp_min_c: 12, temp_max_c: 19` and prints *"Use warmth 2-3 for the base…"*, which is the 16–21°C band. Under `temp_min_c` the same example would have to print *"Outerwear is REQUIRED, warmth 3-4"*, because 12 sits in that band. So the document already chose; it just never said so. The minimum was rejected on its own terms too — it overdresses every day, and on this wardrobe it pushes most of the year into bands `AUDITS.md` O-20 measures as two-item choice sets. The mean was rejected as a number that appears in no document and that a user cannot check against a phone. An hourly reading was rejected because it needs a second endpoint and forces a timezone decision this task otherwise avoids.
**Trade-off accepted:** **A cold morning under a warm afternoon is dressed for the afternoon.** A day of 6°C to 17°C emits the 16–21 rule and no outerwear is required, which is wrong for the commute. Nothing detects this: the rule is correct for the number it was given. The honest mitigations are the coat override at 2.8, which exists precisely so a user can overrule the rule, and `temp_min_c` being present in the response for whoever wants to build a spread-aware rule later. Recorded rather than solved because a two-temperature rule is a change to `03`'s table, not to this function.

## 143 — Open-Meteo's field names and units were verified against a live call, not read from documentation

**Decision:** `app/services/weather.py` requests `temperature_2m_max`, `temperature_2m_min`, `precipitation_sum`, `wind_speed_10m_max` and `weather_code`, with `timezone=auto`. All five names and their units were confirmed by a real request on 2026-08-26 before the parsing code was finalised.
**Alternative:** Write the parser from the provider's documentation and let the first live call find the mismatch.
**Reasoning:** A unit test with a stubbed response asserts our parsing of **our own fixture**, so a wrong field name is invisible to it in both directions — the stub supplies whatever the parser expects. The measurement found one thing memory had wrong: the wind field is **`wind_speed_10m_max`**, and the legacy spelling `windspeed_10m_max` — which is what most examples online still use — returns a `200` with the key simply absent, so the failure is a `KeyError` deep in a service rather than an error from the provider. Units confirmed: °C, mm, **km/h** (not m/s, which is what a mis-parse would silently halve the wind by), and a WMO 4677 integer. `timezone=auto` was confirmed to resolve from the coordinates and return `"timezone": "Asia/Jerusalem"`, which is what makes a calendar date mean the user's day rather than the server's — the timezone gap named at orientation, closed by a parameter rather than by code. The captured body is committed verbatim as the test fixture for the same reason: a hand-written one would have agreed with whatever the parser happened to read.
**Trade-off accepted:** The fixture is a snapshot of one place on one day, so it pins the response *shape* and not the provider's continued behaviour. If Open-Meteo renames a field, the suite stays green and production breaks — nothing in this project can detect that, and no test should be written to pretend otherwise. What the fixture does buy is that a **local** change to the parser fails a test, which is the failure that was actually likely.

## 144 — `condition` is a closed vocabulary of eight, and it lives with the garment vocabularies despite naming the sky

**Decision:** Eight values — `clear · partly_cloudy · cloudy · fog · drizzle · rain · snow · thunderstorm` — added to `02-DATA-MODEL.md`, `app/enums.py` and `frontend/src/app/shared/models/enums.ts` at task 2.1.
**Alternative:** Return Open-Meteo's WMO integer and let the frontend map it; return the provider's English description; or defer the whole question to 2.12, the only screen that renders it.
**Reasoning:** `04-API-SPEC.md` already shows a string (`"partly_cloudy"`), and `GET /weather` ships the field at 2.1, so the value has to be *something* now — deferring was not available. Passing the integer through would put the mapping in the frontend, where the backend already has to have it to be useful to Stage 4's per-day rules. Passing the provider's English description breaks `05-FRONTEND-SPEC.md`'s rule that no user-facing string is hard-coded outside an i18n key, and the descriptions are not a stable contract. What decided the *vocabulary* rather than a free string is the argument that closed every other list in `02`: `partly_cloudy`, `Partly Cloudy` and `partlycloudy` are the same weather and would be three keys. `AUDITS.md` **O-8** makes exactly this case for `occasion` and is still open; this is the same recommendation, taken at the task that first needs it rather than left for the next audit.
**Trade-off accepted:** **A third hand-maintained copy in the seam `141` warns about** — `02` first, `enums.py` second, `enums.ts` third, with nothing comparing any of them. The mitigation is the one thing available: `enums.spec.ts` pins the eight values as a **literal transcribed from `02`** rather than deriving them from the array under test, so adding a value on one side fails on the other. That catches a divergence within the frontend mirror and still cannot compare TypeScript against Python, which remains true of every vocabulary in this project.

## 145 — Coordinates are rounded to two decimals before they become a cache key, and before they leave

**Decision:** `get_forecast` rounds `lat` and `lon` to 2 decimal places, and uses the rounded values both for the cache key and for the request it sends.
**Alternative:** Cache on full precision; round only the key and send full precision; or round to 1 decimal.
**Reasoning:** Two decimals is about 1.1 km, and the measurement that justifies it came from the same live call as `143`: **32.08 was sent and 32.0625 came back** — Open-Meteo snaps to its own grid, coarser than two decimals, so the rounding discards precision the provider was going to discard anyway. Without it the 30-minute cache is decorative: `users.home_lat` is a `REAL` column, so a value that survived a float round-trip would never equal the same place typed into a query string, and every request would miss. Rounding the key but not the request was rejected because it makes the cache dishonest — two nearby coordinates would then share an entry whose contents came from whichever arrived first, at a precision the response no longer describes. One decimal (~11 km) was rejected as merging genuinely different locations in a country this size.
**Trade-off accepted:** Two callers 1.1 km apart across a rounding boundary get two entries and two requests, and two callers 1.1 km apart within one get one. That is a property of any grid and the provider imposes a coarser one regardless. Also accepted: the response echoes the date and the rule but not the coordinates, so a caller cannot see that its input was rounded.

## 146 — An unmapped WMO code falls back to `cloudy` and logs, rather than failing the request

**Decision:** `condition_for` returns `Condition.CLOUDY` for any code outside the twenty-eight WMO 4677 values mapped, and logs a warning carrying the code.
**Alternative:** Raise, and let the route answer `502`; add every code speculatively; or make `condition` nullable.
**Reasoning:** `condition` is a label and an icon. What actually dresses the user is `build_rule`, computed from temperature, rain and wind, and completely unaffected by this field — so failing a whole suggestion over an unknown icon costs the user the feature to protect a decoration. Making it nullable was rejected because `04-API-SPEC.md` shows a string and a null would need a frontend branch no document specifies. What makes the fallback acceptable rather than a silent lie is the log: a widened provider vocabulary is discoverable rather than invisible.
**Trade-off accepted:** **On an unmapped code the API reports weather that is not what the provider said.** The exposure is small — the mapped set is the whole of WMO 4677 as Open-Meteo publishes it — but it is real, and it is the reason the warning carries the integer. Note the log is only readable in production: `JsonFormatter` emits `extra` keys and the development formatter drops them, which is also why the test asserts on the record attribute rather than on `caplog.text`, where a test written the obvious way would pass with the code omitted entirely.

## 147 — `forecast_unavailable` is the first code in this project used at two statuses

**Decision:** `GET /weather` answers `400 forecast_unavailable` when the date is beyond the horizon and `502 forecast_unavailable` when Open-Meteo does not answer. The service raises two exceptions — `ForecastOutOfRangeError` and `ForecastProviderError` — and the route maps them.
**Alternative:** A second code for the provider case; or one status for both.
**Reasoning:** `CONVENTIONS.md`'s code list is otherwise one code to one status, so this is a narrowing of an established pattern and is written down rather than absorbed. What decides it is what the code is *for*: the frontend branches on the code to choose a message, and both cases say the same thing to a user — there is no forecast for that day. The status is for the caller who needs to know whose fault it was, which is a different question and already has a mechanism. A second code would add a string to a list that the frontend would then have to treat identically in both branches. One status for both was rejected because it makes an outage indistinguishable from a bad request in logs and in monitoring. **The `400` covers two paths deliberately:** our own horizon guard, which runs before any network call so the common case costs nothing, and Open-Meteo's own `400` — measured as `{"error": true, "reason": "…out of allowed range…"}` — which arrives for a date older than its archive window and means our request was refused, not that the service is down.
**Trade-off accepted:** A client that branches on status rather than on code sees one code behaving two ways. Pinned by `test_both_failures_share_one_code_at_two_statuses` so that splitting it later is a change to a named test rather than a quiet one.

## 148 — Band thresholds are lower bounds, because the table is whole degrees and the provider answers in decimals

**Decision:** `build_rule` compares `temp_c >= 28`, `>= 22`, `>= 16`, `>= 10`, else the coldest band. A decimal falls into the band below the threshold it does not reach: 27.9 is not 28.
**Alternative:** Round the temperature to an integer before banding; or treat the table's printed ranges as exact and leave the gaps undefined.
**Reasoning:** `03-AI-CONTRACTS.md`'s table is written as `≥ 28`, `22–27`, `16–21`, `10–15`, `< 10` — whole-degree ranges with a gap between every pair. The provider returns one decimal place (31.7 in the captured body), so every gap is reachable and the table does not say what happens in it. Lower bounds are the reading that preserves the table's own `≥ 28` row exactly and makes the other four consistent with it. Rounding first was rejected because it moves the boundary by half a degree in a way the document does not describe: 27.5 would round to 28 and emit *"Do NOT include any outerwear"* for a temperature the table places in the band below.
**Trade-off accepted:** The thresholds now live in code as numbers the document only implies. `test_a_decimal_temperature_stays_in_the_lower_band` pins all four so the reading is asserted rather than assumed, and `test_every_band_emits_the_string_03_specifies` transcribes the five strings from the document rather than deriving them from the module — the self-referential-expectation failure `101` measured at 1.6.

## 149 — `PATCH /me` accepts the whole body, and `display_name` is in it

**Decision:** `PATCH /me` accepts every column `04-API-SPEC.md` prints — `display_name`, `height_cm`, `size_top`, `size_bottom`, `size_shoe`, `style_notes`, `home_city`, `home_lat`, `home_lon` — rather than the three home fields `STAGE-2` 2.2 named. `display_name` can be set and can be cleared.
**Alternative:** The three home fields only, as the stage file said; or the wide body with `display_name` held out of it, as three other sentences said.
**Reasoning:** Four documents gave three answers and `AUDITS.md` **O-6** had already argued the case: `03-AI-CONTRACTS.md`'s stylist prompt carries a `USER PROFILE:` block reading `height_cm` and `style_notes`, `05-FRONTEND-SPEC.md` §8's profile screen is owned by no numbered task, and with a narrow `PATCH` that block is structurally empty in every request this project will ever make. It is the same route and a longer schema, and 2.4 — the task that renders the block — is two tasks away. On `display_name` specifically the documents contradicted themselves rather than each other: `04`'s own worked body carries the field while its prose says *"`PATCH /me` does not set it"*, and **072 contains both halves of the argument two paragraphs apart** — it justifies the `DisplayName` annotation on the grounds that "`PATCH /me` can reuse [it] at Stage 2" and then states in its trade-off that "`PATCH /me` never sets it". The reuse sentence is the one that survives; the trade-off sentence is struck there and in `04`.
**Trade-off accepted:** **A blank display name is now producible through the API for the first time.** Clearing is what "explicit null clears" means and the field is not exempted from it, so a client can write `{"display_name": null}` and the column goes back to what four legacy accounts already have. Nothing breaks — `userLabel` has fallen back to the email address since 0.9 (071), which was written for exactly those rows — but the register form's rule is no longer the last word on the column, and `04`'s sentence about a fresh account never being blank now describes creation only. Also accepted: six of the nine fields have no UI that writes them until someone builds `05` §8, so they are reachable by `curl` and by nothing else. That is the honest state and O-6 is closed on it, rather than closed by building a screen no task owns.

## 150 — `DisplayName` moves to `schemas/user.py`, which 072 did not foresee

**Decision:** The `DisplayName` annotation moves out of `schemas/auth.py` into `schemas/user.py`; `RegisterRequest` imports it from there.
**Alternative:** Import it from `auth.py` into `user.py`; restate the constraint in `UserUpdate`; or put it in a third module.
**Reasoning:** 072 predicted the reuse and the direction of the dependency makes the move compulsory rather than tidy: `auth.py` already imports `UserResponse` from `user.py`, so importing `DisplayName` the other way is a cycle that fails at import. Restating the constraint is the thing 072 exists to prevent — it is a second place to get `StringConstraints`' strip-before-`min_length` ordering wrong. A third module for one type alias is a file with one line in it. 034 already established that `user.py` owns the user shape and that `auth.py` holds only the register and login bodies, so the alias was on the wrong side of a boundary this project had already drawn.
**Trade-off accepted:** A one-line diff in a Stage 0 file, and `git blame` on the register body now points at 2.2 for the annotation rather than at 0.10. The rule's own history stays where it is written — in 072 and in the comment above the alias, which moved with it.

## 151 — The home location is one field in three columns

**Decision:** `home_city`, `home_lat` and `home_lon` are supplied together or cleared together. Any other combination is a `422`, enforced by a model validator on `UserUpdate` that checks both which keys were sent and whether their values agree.
**Alternative:** Three independent nullable fields, as the column definitions suggest; or coordinates required and the city optional.
**Reasoning:** Neither half is usable alone. A `home_city` with no coordinates cannot be given a forecast — `get_forecast` takes numbers — so 2.7 would read a home location, find no coordinates, and have to treat a populated row as absent. Coordinates with no city name give 2.12's strip a temperature with nothing to label it, on a screen whose whole job is to say *where*. The document supports this by omission rather than by statement: `04` calls them "the default location for daily looks", singular. Independent fields were rejected because they make a half-written location a state every later reader has to handle; requiring coordinates and leaving the city optional was rejected because the city is the only human-readable part and the geocoder returns all three together anyway.
**Trade-off accepted:** A client cannot correct a typo in a city name without re-sending both coordinates, which is a real cost for a hand-written `curl` and none at all for the search endpoint's output, where the three arrive as one object. **The rule needed two checks and only one of them is reachable in most requests** — found by mutation: deleting the count check left the suite green, because every partial request the tests sent was already refused by the value check. The one request that reaches it is `{"home_city": null}` on its own, and there is now a test named for exactly that.

## 152 — `geocoding_unavailable` is its own code rather than a widened `forecast_unavailable`

**Decision:** A `502` from `GET /me/locations/search` carries `code: "geocoding_unavailable"`, added to `CONVENTIONS.md`'s list.
**Alternative:** Reuse `forecast_unavailable`; or reuse the generic `not_found`/`upload_failed` shape with a new status only.
**Reasoning:** The codes in this project name the condition, not the vendor and not the endpoint — `email_exists`, `file_too_large`, `wardrobe_too_small`. Two Open-Meteo services fail independently and mean different things to the person reading the screen: one is *we cannot look up that place*, the other is *we have no weather for that day*. Reusing `forecast_unavailable` would make the code mean "an Open-Meteo call failed", which is a fact about our supplier rather than about the user's request, and 147 spent a whole entry narrowing that code to two statuses of one condition — widening it now to a second condition would undo that in the same stage.
**Trade-off accepted:** One more string the frontend has to know, on a list that is already fourteen long, for a branch no screen renders yet. The endpoint has no caller until Stage 4's trip form or a profile screen, so the code is written down before anything reads it — deliberately, because 147's precedent says a code's meaning is decided when it is minted rather than when it is first rendered.

## 153 — Four keys, wrapped, and an empty list is an answer

**Decision:** `GET /me/locations/search` returns `{"results": [...]}` with `name`, `country`, `lat` and `lon` per entry, at most five; a query matching nothing is a `200` with `results: []`; `q` is trimmed and must be at least two characters, below which it is a `422` and no request leaves the process.
**Alternative:** A bare JSON array; a `404` on no match; returning the provider's `admin1` as a fifth key; a longer minimum.
**Reasoning:** `04-API-SPEC.md` specified the four keys and the count and nothing else, so the envelope, the empty case and the floor were all decided here. The wrapper follows `GET /items`: a top-level array has nowhere to put a second key, and this endpoint is the kind that eventually grows one. A `404` was rejected because the caller is a type-ahead — every keystroke that has not finished spelling a city would be an error, and `CONVENTIONS.md` requires every failure path to carry a written message, which "you are still typing" is not. Two characters is the **provider's** floor rather than a house rule, measured from its documented behaviour: one character matches nothing at all and two match only exactly. Trimming before measuring is 072's ordering lesson applied to a query string — `"  "` is two characters and no place.
**Trade-off accepted:** **`name` plus `country` does not identify a place uniquely, and the live call proves it** — a search for `berlin` returns Berlin, Germany and Berlin, New Jersey, and this response cannot tell them apart in a picker. `admin1` would fix it and is deliberately not returned: `04` is authoritative over the wire shape, and widening a documented contract because it would be convenient is the decision this project asks to be made deliberately rather than in passing. Recorded here so that whoever builds the profile screen or the trip form can reopen it with the evidence in hand instead of rediscovering it.

## 154 — Geocoding is its own module, and it is not cached

**Decision:** `app/services/geocoding.py`, separate from `weather.py`, with no cache of any kind.
**Alternative:** A `search_locations()` function inside `weather.py`, which already owns an Open-Meteo client; or the same 30-minute cache `get_forecast` uses.
**Reasoning:** The two share a vendor and nothing else — a different host, a different response shape, different failure semantics, and no coordinate rounding. `weather.py` is already 280 lines and its docstring is entirely about forecasts and rules. `01-ARCHITECTURE.md`'s tree lists services individually, so a new file costs one line in a document that was going to need editing anyway. On the cache: 145 justified the forecast cache with a measurement — the provider snaps coordinates to a grid, so two nearby requests are genuinely the same request — and none of that transfers. A type-ahead sends `b`, `be`, `ber`, `berl` and `berli` on the way to `berlin`, each of them once, so a cache would hold entries that are asked for exactly one time each and evict the one entry that might repeat.
**Trade-off accepted:** A second module implementing the same transport-override seam, five lines of it, duplicated rather than shared. Extracting a common HTTP helper was refused as the kind of abstraction that reads as tidy and makes both call sites harder to follow; if a third Open-Meteo client ever appears, that is the moment to reconsider. Also accepted: a user who types the same city twice in a session pays for two requests against a free, keyless API.

## 155 — The height bounds live in the model and the schema imports them

**Decision:** `MIN_HEIGHT_CM` and `MAX_HEIGHT_CM` are defined in `app/models/user.py`; the `CheckConstraint` is built from them with an f-string, and `UserUpdate` imports them for its `Field(ge=…, le=…)`.
**Alternative:** Transcribe `120` and `230` into the schema as literals, the way every other mirrored limit in this project is written; or leave the schema unbounded and let Postgres refuse the value.
**Reasoning:** Leaving it to Postgres is the one option that is simply wrong: an `IntegrityError` escaping a route is a `500` with no `code`, which is the shape 043 added `upload_failed` to close, and it would arrive for a request a schema can refuse in one line. Between the other two, `CONVENTIONS.md`'s "Limits and units" section exists because a limit honoured in two places drifts silently — and every instance it records is a Python value hand-copied into TypeScript, where nothing *can* compare them. Here both enforcers are Python in one package, so the copy is avoidable, and an avoidable copy is not a cost worth accepting for symmetry with cases that had no choice. The generated constraint string is byte-identical to the literal it replaces, so migration `0001` — which spells `ck_users_height_cm_range` out by hand — is untouched and stays the frozen definition it should be.
**Trade-off accepted:** `schemas/user.py` now imports from `app.models.user`, which is a direction this project had not used before — `schemas/item.py` imports from `services/storage.py`, so schemas reaching sideways is established, but reaching into models is new. The alternative was a third copy of two integers. The migration's literal remains a genuine hand-copy that nothing compares against the model, and that is correct: a migration describes the database as it was on the day it ran.

## 156 — The wardrobe line is positional, carries a second colour, and its token cost is measured rather than estimated

**Decision:** `serialize_wardrobe` writes every core slot on every line, using an em dash (`—`, U+2014) where the item has no value, and omits only the trailing extras (`rise:{value}`, then `water_resistant`). A `color_secondary` slot is added to the format immediately after the primary colour; `display_name` stays out. The token budget is **measured**: 30.7 tokens per item, **4,604 tokens for 150 items** (min 4,588, max 4,617 across 20 runs — ±0.3%), counted with `tiktoken` against the encoding `settings.OPENAI_STYLIST_MODEL` resolves to (`gpt-4o-mini-2024-07-18` → `o200k_base`). The unit test's ceiling stays at **6,000**.
**Alternative:** Omit nulls literally, as `STAGE-2` 2.3's one-line summary says; keep the single-colour format; keep any of the three estimates already written down; set the ceiling from a headroom ratio below the measurement.
**Reasoning:** **The line is positional and the model is given no key**, so an omitted middle field is not a saving — it shifts every later value one column left and a material is read as a pattern. `03-AI-CONTRACTS.md`'s own worked example had shown the em dash for a shoe with no `fit` since Stage 0, so the two documents disagreed and the example was the one that had thought about it; the rule is now written out rather than left to be inferred from four sample lines. Omission is still correct at the end of the line, where there is no later column to shift. **`color_secondary` earns its place on measurement rather than on principle**: colour coordination is the stylist's core function, 9 of the 64 committed seed rows are two-tone, and the slot costs 303 tokens across 150 items — about 2 an item, 7% — which is a small price for not describing one item in seven wrongly. This is the cheapest moment to change the format, before 2.4 builds a prompt on it and 2.5 validates against it. **`display_name` is refused on the opposite arithmetic**: free text beside a closed vocabulary, buying a name the model can already construct from `subcategory` plus two colours. **On the ceiling:** the number this task was given was 6,000, and the headroom rule proposed alongside it — 30% *under* 6,000, i.e. ≤4,200 — turned out to be the wrong ratio and jointly unsatisfiable with the 150-item test it sat beside, since 4,200 at the real density is 136 items. What a guard rail needs is margin **above** real output, and 6,000 is about 30% above the measured 4,604 and above the across-run maximum of 4,617, with ±0.3% variance. That is a wide, non-flaky ceiling. **All three prior estimates were wrong in the same direction as each other's disagreement:** `03` said ~28/item and ~4,200 (9% low), 002 said ~4,000 (13% low), `01-ARCHITECTURE.md` said ~7,000 (52% high). One canonical figure now lives in `03` and the other two quote it, because three independent estimates of one number is how they came to disagree in the first place. The size curve is 64 → 1,962 · 150 → 4,606 · 200 → 6,144 · 400 → 12,266, which **reaffirms 002**: the ~400-item ceiling is about 12,300 tokens, comfortably inside context, so no-retrieval-layer is a product judgement and not something the arithmetic forces.
**Trade-off accepted:** **The em dash costs a token per absent field** — one per line on most items, three on a bag — where omission would cost nothing; that is the price of a format the model can read positionally, and it is inside the measurement above. **`tiktoken` enters `requirements.txt` for a test and nothing under `app/` imports it**, which is a first for this project. It is also a deliberate exception to the no-network-in-tests norm: `encoding_for_model` downloads the BPE table on first use and caches it on disk — observed here as 0 → 1 files in `$TMPDIR/data-gym-cache`, 3.43s cold against 0.287s warm — so the first run on any new machine, and every run in a CI container with no cache, reaches `openaipublic.blob.core.windows.net`. `TIKTOKEN_CACHE_DIR` pointed at a committed or restored cache is the mechanism that makes it offline, and that belongs to whoever writes `ci.yml` under **O-19**. **The measurement is of the committed demo wardrobe cycled to 150**, not of a synthetic fixture: it is the honest number for this project's data and it would move for a wardrobe of longer subcategory words. **And `short_id` is not upper-cased in the serialiser** — `app/core/short_id.py`'s alphabet has no lower case, so `.upper()` could never change a character, and `STAGE-2` 2.3's "uppercase `short_id`" is satisfied by construction. Normalising what the *model* returns is a different problem and belongs to `validate_look_response` at 2.5.

## 157 — The stylist schema is single-day, and three of `03`'s tokens do not survive strict mode

**Decision:** `STYLIST_SCHEMA` in `app/services/stylist.py` is `{"name": "outfit_recommendation", "strict": true}` over three root properties — `looks`, `missing_pieces`, `message`. A look carries six: `day`, `occasion`, `title`, `item_ids`, `reasoning`, `weather_note`. **`confidence` and `look_id` are struck**, **`packing_list` (with its `by_category` map) is deferred to Stage 4**, and **`minItems` is not used anywhere**. `required` is derived from the property dict rather than restated. `03-AI-CONTRACTS.md`'s response block is edited to match, with the deferral written into it so the document and the code read as staging rather than disagreement.
**Alternative:** Build `03`'s block as printed, with `packing_list` nullable and emitted as `null` on every single-day call, and `by_category` narrowed to seven fixed integer keys. Keep `confidence` and `look_id` because the document shows them. Use `minItems: 1` to make "at least one look" a schema guarantee instead of a validation rule.
**Reasoning:** **Strict mode is what forces the question**: every property must appear in `required`, so there is no such thing as an optional field — a field in the schema is a field the model produces, and pays for, on every call. `confidence` had already lost this argument once. 086 refused the vision `confidence` a branch on the evidence that eight live responses returned `0.9` including both wrong ones, and `AUDITS.md` **O-9** asked for the same decision here *before* a schema was built from the document. `look_id` fails the same test one field along: a model-invented string with no column in `02`, no renderer in `05` and no task, where `day` already keys a look to its day. **`packing_list` is deferred rather than struck, and for a reason the strict-mode rules make concrete**: `by_category` is a free-form `{category: count}` map, and strict mode requires `additionalProperties: false` with every key named, so it cannot be expressed as written. Turning it into a fixed list of keys is a decision, and the task that should make it is the one with a reader for it — packing is Stage 4's headline feature, and its schema belongs beside the trip user message and the reuse arithmetic rather than guessed two stages early as a field that is `null` on every call this project makes. **`minItems` is absent because it is unverified**, and an unsupported keyword is a `400` that reads as a model problem; "one look per expected day" is rule 4 of `03`'s validation table, which is 2.5's and can say what was wrong.
**Trade-off accepted:** `03` now describes a schema that is a subset of what `suggest_looks` will eventually send, which is a state this project usually treats as a defect — mitigated by saying so in the document at the point of difference, with the task that closes it named. `day` survives with no reader at 2.4 and immediately proved slippery: the live verification call answered `"day": 14` for a request dated 2026-03-14, which is **O-24** and a decision for 2.5. And a test pins the six-key list, so a later tidy that "adds back what the document shows" fails rather than quietly costing tokens on every call.

## 158 — Three sentences added to the stylist prompt, and two of them close a gap `04` had been carrying since Stage 0

**Decision:** `app/prompts/stylist_system.md` is `03-AI-CONTRACTS.md`'s prompt verbatim plus three sentences in CONSTRAINTS: the wardrobe-cannot-satisfy fallback (*"dress the day from the closest items it does hold … Never refuse to build a look"*), the outerwear precedence (*"An explicit outerwear instruction from the user overrides the weather rule. Where none is given, the weather rule decides."*), and a clause on the existing shortfall bullet (*"`missing_pieces` is a note beside a complete look and never a replacement for one."*). The user message gains matching `Notes:` and `Outerwear:` lines, both omitted entirely when the field is null. `03` is edited to carry all of it.
**Alternative:** Leave the prompt verbatim and let the model resolve the conflict itself. Put the outerwear preference beside the occasion instead of after the weather rule. Have 2.7 or 2.8 add the prompt slots when the fields first arrive on the wire.
**Reasoning:** **`04-API-SPEC.md` has specified `notes` and `include_outerwear` on `POST /looks/suggest` since Stage 0, and `03`'s user message had a line for neither** — two fields on the wire with nowhere to go in the only message that reaches the model. 2.4 is the only task that owns prompt assembly, so deferring the slots would mean 2.7 and 2.8 reopening this file. **The precedence sentence is what makes `include_outerwear` mean anything**: the prompt already said the weather rule "is not a suggestion", so a `false` from a user who does not want a coat at 12°C was an instruction the model had been told to ignore. **The fallback sentence resolves a contradiction that was already in the document** — line 292 says obey the rule exactly, the shortfall bullet says still return your best look, and on a wardrobe with no qualifying item those point opposite ways. `AUDITS.md` **O-20** makes this concrete rather than theoretical: zero of the 64 seed rows are `water_resistant` and the 22–27°C band has no qualifying items, so the demo wardrobe *cannot* satisfy two branches of the rule table and the honest instruction is to approximate rather than to fail. **Printed after the weather rule, not beside the occasion**, because a reader takes the later of two conflicting instructions as the operative one; the ordering on the page agrees with the precedence in words.
**Trade-off accepted:** Three sentences of prompt is three sentences a future reader must diff against `03` by eye, which is the cost of a prompt that is a file rather than a rendered template — `vision_system.md` avoids that for the vocabulary only because `enums.py` generates it. Tests pin all three literally, so deleting one fails a named test rather than degrading behaviour silently. **The ANCHOR and LOCKED conditionals are transcribed verbatim and are inert until 2.10 and 2.11**: they cost a few tokens per call and let the file be compared to the document line for line, which is worth more than deleting and restoring them twice.

## 159 — `USE_FAKE_AI` builds the look out of the wardrobe it was handed, because a recorded fixture cannot work here

**Decision:** `stylist._fake_response` selects real `short_id`s from the wardrobe passed to `suggest_looks` — first shoes, then a top and a bottom, falling back to the first dress when there is no pair — and returns them with `reasoning`, `weather_note` and `message` that say "Placeholder" out loud. No styling, no weather, no fixture file. `STAGE-2` 2.4's "recorded fixture" wording and `06-TESTING-STRATEGY.md`'s fake-AI section are both corrected to match.
**Alternative:** A committed JSON fixture with literal ids, as `STAGE-2` 2.4 and `06` both say. A fake that reads the weather rule and picks by `warmth`, so the flag exercises more of the behaviour.
**Reasoning:** **A fixture with literal ids cannot work, and the reason is arithmetic rather than taste.** `scripts/seed_demo.py` calls `generate_short_id()` per row, so the demo wardrobe's ids differ on every seed; a fixture naming `A3F9K2` names an item that does not exist in the database it is running against, and 2.5's rule 1 — the hallucination guard, the rule that "must never be relaxed" — would answer `502` to every call made with the flag on. `06`'s E2E journeys 6 and 7 exist to *see a look card* and to assert that every item in it is real, so a fake that can only produce a `502` makes both of them assert the opposite of what they are for, and `07-DEPLOYMENT.md` runs the whole E2E job with `USE_FAKE_AI=true`. This is 081's shape a second time — a documented rule broken deliberately, with the reason written down and a name on it — and the mitigation is 081's too: the text says it is a placeholder, so a demo accidentally run with the flag on is visible on screen rather than passing for a real look. **Deliberately not clever**: a fake that reasoned about warmth would be a second, untested stylist, and the flag exists to be deterministic rather than good.
**Trade-off accepted:** Every fake look is the same three garments in the same order, so nothing under the flag exercises variety — the same cost 081 accepted for forty identical white shirts, and acceptable for the same reason: what the flag buys is determinism. The fake also ignores `include_outerwear`, so a Playwright journey that sets the coat override to *Yes* will not see outerwear; that is a real limit on journey design and is recorded here rather than discovered at 5.3. Task 5.1 may still record a real response for the *shape*, but it can never record the ids.

## 160 — Two model pins, one per contract

**Decision:** `app/core/config.py` keeps `OPENAI_MODEL` for vision and gains `OPENAI_STYLIST_PIN` for the stylist. Both are `gpt-4o-mini-2024-07-18` today; `OPENAI_VISION_MODEL` and `OPENAI_STYLIST_MODEL` default from one each and remain overridable by environment. `01`, `03`, `07`, `docs/README.md` and `.env.example` are corrected — four documents said the model was pinned in one place.
**Alternative:** Keep one constant, which is what 078 decided and what every document said. Rename `OPENAI_MODEL` to `OPENAI_VISION_PIN` for symmetry.
**Reasoning:** `PROGRESS.md` recorded this as a silent coupling when Stage 1 closed, and named 2.4 as the place to decide it. **Task 1.11 is chartered to re-pin the vision model against `gpt-5.4-mini-2026-03-17` and measure the difference on a golden set of photographs.** While one constant served both, that re-pin also changed the model behind every acceptance criterion in Stage 2 — a stage whose criteria are *entirely* about how the stylist behaves ("forcing 12°C produces outerwear", "`reasoning` explains the combination"). One constant cannot carry two independent decisions, and the failure mode is the worst kind: a Stage 2 observation invalidated by a Stage 1 task, with nothing on screen to say so. 078's reasoning is untouched — dated snapshot, one variable at a time, overridable by environment — and this splits *what* it applies to, not *why*.
**Trade-off accepted:** Two constants holding the same string is a duplicate that a tidy-minded reader will want to collapse, which is why the comment beside it says what it is for. **The names are asymmetric** — `OPENAI_MODEL` and `OPENAI_STYLIST_PIN` — because renaming the vision constant would touch four documents and `DECISIONS.md` 078 for cosmetic gain; the asymmetry is the cost of not rewriting history, and a later task that wants symmetry can rename both in one commit. A test asserts the stylist call ignores `OPENAI_VISION_MODEL`, so collapsing them again fails a named test.

## 161 — What escapes the OpenAI SDK is measured, and four failures that are not `OpenAIError` become one `ValueError`

**Decision:** `suggest_looks` reads the answer through `_content` and `_build`, which raise `ValueError` for **no choices, `choices=None`, a non-`ChatCompletion` return value, `content=None`, unparseable JSON, and JSON that is not the documented shape** — and let every provider exception escape untouched. `_content` uses `getattr(completion, "choices", None)` rather than attribute access, against the SDK's own type annotation.
**Alternative:** `except OpenAIError` around the call, which is what the SDK documents and what reading suggests. Trust `strict: true` to make a malformed answer impossible. Let the builtin exceptions escape as they are, since 2.7 will catch something anyway.
**Reasoning:** Measured against **openai 2.52.0** with a local HTTP server, before the code was written, the way 044 established for Cloudinary and 143 for Open-Meteo. The transport and status failures behave as documented — `APIConnectionError`, `APITimeoutError`, and `BadRequestError`/`RateLimitError`/`InternalServerError` under `APIStatusError`, all under `OpenAIError`. **Three do not.** A `200` whose body is not JSON raises `json.JSONDecodeError` *from inside the SDK* — a `ValueError`, not an `OpenAIError`. A `200` carrying `text/html` makes `create()` return a **`str`**. A `200` carrying JSON of the wrong shape returns a `ChatCompletion` whose `choices` is `None`, which the SDK's own annotation says cannot happen; `choices: []` returns as-is. Left alone, those surface as `AttributeError`, `TypeError` and `IndexError` from inside a route — the bare `500` with no `code` that `CONVENTIONS.md` promises the frontend never sees, which is exactly the hole 043 added `upload_failed` to close on the Cloudinary path. Funnelling them into the `ValueError` this module already documents means 2.7 catches **two** things — *no usable answer* and *the provider did not answer* — which is 086's split between `TaggingError` and a provider exception, applied to the second contract.
**Trade-off accepted:** A `getattr` on an object whose type says the attribute is always there reads as superstition, and needs the comment beside it to say it was measured; the same is true of the `not choices` check. The distinction between the two failure kinds only pays off at 2.7, so until then it looks like ceremony. **And the same three holes exist in `vision.py`, unguarded** — `tag_item` does `completion.choices[0].message` — where they are contained by `tag_and_store`'s `except Exception` (088) rather than by design. Opened as **O-23** rather than fixed here, because `vision.py` belongs to 1.2b and 082 refused exactly this kind of ride-along one stage earlier.

## 162 — `StylistContext` in, frozen dataclasses out, and no `Session` anywhere near this module

**Decision:** `suggest_looks(wardrobe: Sequence[ItemResponse], context: StylistContext, correction: str | None = None) -> StylistResponse`. `StylistContext` is a frozen slotted dataclass carrying `date`, `occasion`, `forecast_summary`, `weather_rule`, `notes`, `include_outerwear`, `height_cm` and `style_notes`, built by 2.7. The answer is frozen slotted dataclasses too — `StylistResponse`, `Look`, `MissingPiece`. The module imports `serialize_wardrobe` and calls it itself.
**Alternative:** Take the already-serialised string from 2.7. Take the `User` row and the `Forecast` and read the fields here. Pydantic models for the answer, or `client.chat.completions.parse()` with a Pydantic model generating the schema.
**Reasoning:** **Items in rather than a string** because the fake needs real `short_id`s to select from (159) and the `WARDROBE ({n} items)` header needs a count; both are free from a sequence and awkward from a blob of text. **A context object rather than the `User` row and the `Forecast`** because it keeps a `Session` and a clock out of this module — the weather rule arrives already computed by `build_rule`, which is what 004 bought and what makes the weather behaviour testable without an AI call. **Frozen dataclasses rather than Pydantic**, following `ItemTags` at 086: Pydantic is this project's wire-body type, the wire body for a look is 2.7's *hydrated* shape where `item_ids` have become full items, and a model here would validate the same shape a second time under rules that are not the schema's. `.parse()` is refused for the reason `vision.py` already records — it builds a schema from a Pydantic model, and what a live call verified is the schema `03-AI-CONTRACTS.md` wrote down. **`correction` is a parameter** because 2.5 owns the retry and a violation has to reach the model somehow; it is 1.2b's `tag_item(image_url, correction=…)` shape, and the `03` sentence it formats is transcribed rather than imported from `vision.py`, so a change to one contract's retry is not a silent change to the other's.
**Trade-off accepted:** `StylistContext` is defined in the service and constructed by a route, which is the reverse of this project's usual direction — `schemas/` holds the shapes routes build. It is not a wire shape and never reaches a client, so a Pydantic model in `schemas/` would have been a request body that no request sends. Two of its eight fields (`notes`, `include_outerwear`) have no caller until 2.7 and no UI until 2.8, which is a deliberate exception to the no-speculative-parameters rule: `04-API-SPEC.md` specifies both, and the alternative was reopening the prompt assembly twice (158).

## 163 — `day` is struck from the look object, and O-24 is closed by removal rather than by instruction

**Decision:** `day` leaves `STYLIST_SCHEMA`, the `Look` dataclass and `_fake_response`. A look object carries five properties — `occasion`, `title`, `item_ids`, `reasoning`, `weather_note`. `03-AI-CONTRACTS.md`'s response example and its narrowings list are edited to match, and Stage 4 reintroduces a day number beside the trip schema that has a reader for it. `AUDITS.md` **O-24** closes.
**Alternative:** O-24 recorded three. Keep `day` and print `Day: 1` in the single-day user message, so the model is told rather than left to infer. Keep it and add a validation rule that looks are numbered `1..n`. Or keep it untouched, since one look is one look whatever it is numbered.
**Reasoning:** This is 157's test applied a second time to the field that only just survived it. `confidence` and `look_id` were struck at 2.4 because in strict mode every property is one the model must produce on every call, and neither had a column, a renderer or a task; `day` was kept on the strength of one sentence — *it is what keys a look to its day at Stage 4* — and **that sentence is not what `02-DATA-MODEL.md` says.** The `looks` table has `for_date` and `trip_id`. There is no day column at Stage 2, none at Stage 3, and none in the Stage 4 DDL either, so "it becomes real at 2.6 if look persistence writes it to a column" is answered: 2.6 does not. **What measurement there is points the other way too.** The single live call at 2.4 answered `"day": 14` for a request dated 2026-03-14 while `USE_FAKE_AI` answered `1` — the model and the fake disagreed about what the field meant, and the fake was the one that was right. Of the three options, the instruction (*print `Day: 1`*) does not survive a model that ignores it, and the validation rule spends the project's one retry on a number nothing reads, in a table whose rules 7 and 8 are already spoken for by 2.10 and 2.11. Removing the field removes the question. **Stage 4 is a better place to answer it** because that is where a day number acquires a meaning — one look per day of a trip, mapped onto `for_date` — and where the trip user message can state the numbering in the same breath as the schema.
**Trade-off accepted:** **Stage 4 has to add it back**, which is a schema change and a `Look` field in a task that already carries the packing list; that is the same shape as 157's `packing_list` deferral and is accepted for the same reason — a field designed beside its reader is cheaper than a field carried for two stages with none. This is also the second time 2.4's schema has been reopened, and the honest reading is that O-9's question was asked one field short. The four tests at 2.4 that named `day` move with it rather than being deleted: the six-key pin becomes a five-key pin, and `test_the_response_is_immutable` freezes `occasion` instead.

## 164 — `validate_look_response` judges, and 2.7 retries: five rules, the first violation, and the normalised response

**Decision:** `validate_look_response(response, wardrobe, context) -> LookValidation` in `app/services/stylist.py`. Synchronous; it calls nothing, raises nothing and ships no exception class. `LookValidation` carries `response` — the answer with every returned id `.upper()`ed — and `violation`, the **first** failing rule worded for the model, or `None`, with an `ok` property. Five of `03`'s eight rules run, in `03`'s order: 1, 2, 3, 4, 6. Rule 3 is read by `layer`; rule 1 is checked against the wardrobe that was **sent**; rule 6 does not run when `include_outerwear is False` and asks its question through a new `weather.requires_outerwear(rule)` built from the band constants themselves. One `logger.warning` names the violation and is pinned by a test that reads the record attribute rather than the rendered text. The retry, the give-up log and `ApiError(502, "stylist_failed")` are 2.7's.
**Alternative:** 1.2b's shape exactly — an `async validate_look_response` owning its own retry and raising a `StylistError` the route maps, which is what `validate_tags(raw, image_url)` does on the first contract. Report every violation joined rather than the first. Normalise ids in a separate exported function, or in `_build` at 2.4. Ask rule 6 its question with `"REQUIRED" in rule`, or by re-reading the temperature. Run rule 6 unconditionally, as `03`'s table prints it.
**Reasoning:** **The retry is where the two contracts genuinely differ.** `validate_tags` can re-call the model because a retry needs one extra argument — the image URL. Re-calling the stylist needs the whole wardrobe and the whole context, so a validator holding those would *be* the orchestrator 2.7 is chartered to be, and every one of `STAGE-2` 2.5's "hand-built response object" unit tests would have become `async` for nothing. Sync and pure is also what makes the rules testable at all before `POST /looks/suggest` exists. **The first violation rather than a list** because it is prompt text, not a report: `03` specifies an order, and one concrete instruction is likelier to be obeyed than five. The order is load-bearing rather than cosmetic — rule 1 is what makes every later rule able to look an id up, so it runs first and returns before the rest can `KeyError` on a hallucinated id. **Normalisation returns with the verdict** so there is exactly one place an id can change case; 2.7 persists and hydrates from `LookValidation.response`, and cannot use the raw ids by accident. It is `.upper()` and nothing else: 156 assigned exactly that here, nothing measured pads an id, and transliteration is impossible by construction — `short_id`'s alphabet drops *both* halves of every confusable pair, so a returned `0` maps to no legal character and is a hallucination rather than a typo. **Rule 6 is narrowed because 158 already decided the precedence**: an explicit `include_outerwear: false` overrides the weather rule and the prompt says so in words, so enforcing rule 6 over a look that obeyed the user at 12°C would spend the retry and then answer `502` to the one answer that did as it was told. **`requires_outerwear` lives in `weather.py`** and is a frozenset of the two band constants rather than a substring test or a re-typed pair of sentences: the module that owns the table is the one that knows which bands demand a coat, and a reworded band moves both at once. `startswith`, because the rain and wind modifiers are appended — and rain is exactly when the cold bands fire.
**Trade-off accepted:** **A function named `validate…` that also returns data** reads oddly, and the docstring has to say why; the alternative was two calls at 2.7 where forgetting the first fails silently at hydration. **The two contracts' validators now have different shapes**, which is a real inconsistency for a reader who learns one and expects the other — recorded here rather than smoothed over, because the asymmetry is in the retries, not in the taste. **Rule 4 hard-codes 1** rather than taking an `expected_days` parameter no caller can supply until Stage 4; `03`'s wording is `len(looks) == expected_days` and this is the single-day half of it. And **`stylist.py` now imports `weather.py`** — the first edge between the two Stage 2 services, harmless today (weather imports only `enums`) and worth knowing about before anything in `weather.py` reaches back.

## 165 — `0002` is the printed DDL minus three columns, and the ORM models land with the migration

**Decision:** `alembic/versions/0002_looks.py` creates `looks` and `look_items` exactly as `02-DATA-MODEL.md` prints them, minus `feedback` and `worn_at` (migration `0003`) and `trip_id` (migration `0004`, which also creates the table it references). `app/models/look.py` lands in the same commit with `Look` and `LookItem`, carrying the same three omissions, no `relationship()` anywhere, and no index beyond the two primary keys. Nothing writes a row: `POST /looks/suggest` at 2.7 is the first writer, and it writes from a route the way `items.py` does.
**Alternative:** Migration alone here, models at 2.7 with their first caller. `relationship()` between `Look`, `LookItem` and `Item`, so a look could be read as an object graph. Create `looks (user_id)` and `look_items (item_id)` now. Ship a `persist_look()` service function for 2.7 to call.
**Reasoning:** **The models belong to this task because 077 built a test that only fires now.** `tests/unit/test_db_naming.py` compares `Base.metadata`'s constraint names against a hand-maintained list of what the migrations spell, and that list was written knowing it would have to be extended when `looks` and `look_items` landed — it goes red the moment the models are imported, deliberately, and a task that ships the migration without them defers the one check that catches a table named under two conventions. **No relationships** follows `Item`, which names `user_id` and stops: 2.7 hydrates from the wardrobe it already holds in memory, and Stage 3's preference block is plain SQL aggregation by its own stage file, so an object graph would be built for no reader. **No extra indexes** because `02` prints none and this project does not invent schema; the composite primary key already serves every lookup by `look_id`, which is the only direction anything reads before Stage 3. Both foreseeable indexes are recorded in `AUDITS.md` **O-25** rather than built on two empty tables. **No `persist_look()`** because `01-ARCHITECTURE.md` lists no looks service, routes in this project write their own rows, and a helper with one caller that does not exist yet is a shape guessed a task early.
**Trade-off accepted:** **The model and the printed DDL differ by three columns**, so a reader comparing them finds a discrepancy that is correct — `02` explains it in prose, and this is the second time (`items.wear_count` at 0.7 was the first). The hand-maintained name list now carries thirteen entries and must be extended again at `0003` and `0004`; that is the cost 077 already accepted, and it fails loudly rather than silently. And `downgrade()` drops two tables and nothing else — verified by running it against the test database, which is also what made this task's mutation run possible at all.

## 166 — `role` and `position` are built as printed and land unwritten, and the vocabulary is not invented here

**Decision:** `look_items.role` is `TEXT`, nullable, with no `CHECK` and no enum type; `position` is `SMALLINT NOT NULL DEFAULT 0`. Nothing writes either column at 2.6, and no `Role` vocabulary is added to `enums.py`, to `enums.ts` or to `02-DATA-MODEL.md`. `AUDITS.md` **O-25** is opened for the vocabulary and for the two deferred indexes, and 2.7 — the first writer — decides.
**Alternative:** Name the six values `02`'s comment carries as a `Role` enum now and `CHECK` the column against them. Derive the value from `Category` at 2.6 and write a map. Or drop both columns until a task writes one, and add them in a later migration.
**Reasoning:** **The six values cannot be adopted without a decision that is not this task's**, and reading them closely is what shows it: `02`'s comment lists `top · bottom · outer · shoes · bag · accessory`, `Category` has seven members, **`dress` maps to none of them**, and `outerwear` and `outer` are the same garment class under two spellings. `04-API-SPEC.md` gives `replace_role` the same six, so the gap is on the wire at 2.11 as well. Nothing produces a role today — `STYLIST_SCHEMA` returns ids — so the value is derived server-side or it is null, and that is a choice about vocabulary, which this project makes in `02` first (O-8's `occasion` is the same shape, one column along). **Building the column as printed** keeps `0002` faithful to the authoritative DDL, which is the one rule a migration cannot bend, while leaving the naming to the task that first has a value to write. **Dropping the columns** would mean a second migration at 2.11 for a column `02` already prints, and a table that disagrees with the document for two stages.
**Trade-off accepted:** **Two columns land that nothing reads and nothing writes** — precisely what `CONVENTIONS.md` forbids in application code, accepted here because the DDL is authoritative and the alternative is editing `02` on a guess. The composite primary key also means `role` can never distinguish two rows for the same item in one look, so a future "worn as both" is a schema change and not a value change. And a nullable `role` with no constraint will accept `"Outer"` or `"outerwear"` or anything else the first writer happens to send: **the guard against that is O-25 being answered before 2.7 writes a value**, not the column.

## 167 — Swimwear and sleepwear are categories, appended, and `0003` renumbers the two migrations behind it

**Decision:** `Category` gains `swimwear` and `sleepwear` as its eighth and ninth members, **appended after `accessory`** in `02-DATA-MODEL.md`, `app/enums.py` and `enums.ts` alike. `SUBCATEGORIES` gives swimwear `swimsuit · bikini · swim_shorts · cover_up · rash_guard` and sleepwear `pajamas · nightdress · robe`; `FIELD_APPLIES_TO` admits both for `fit` and `length` and neither for `rise`; `_SLEEVED` and `_HEMMED` take both, while `VALUE_APPLIES_TO["fit"]` is untouched; `LAYERS_BY_CATEGORY` gives both `admits={standalone}, answer=standalone`. Migration **`0003_vocabulary`** runs two `ALTER TYPE item_category ADD VALUE IF NOT EXISTS` statements inside Alembic's transaction and has a `downgrade()` that deliberately does nothing. Stage 3's feedback becomes `0004` and Stage 4's trips `0005`. Ten keys are added to `frontend/public/i18n/en.json`. `AUDITS.md` **O-21** is half closed; the filter is 2.7's.
**Alternative:** Ship the exclusion list configurable over `(category, subcategory)` pairs and **empty**, keeping the promise structurally true at no vocabulary cost. Or strike the promise from `01-ARCHITECTURE.md` and `STAGE-2` 2.4 and say the wardrobe goes in whole, which is what the code already does and what 002 argues for everywhere except that one sentence. Both were O-21's second and third options and both are smaller than this.
**Reasoning:** The choice was made as a **product** decision rather than a documentation repair: a person photographing their wardrobe uploads a swimsuit and a pair of pyjamas, and an app that cannot name them either mis-tags them as `top` and `bottom` — where the stylist will then dress someone for the office in them — or refuses the upload. The empty-list option keeps a promise true by making it do nothing, and the strike option answers the user's swimsuit with a wrong tag. **Appended rather than inserted** because `ALTER TYPE … ADD VALUE` appends to the PostgreSQL type's sort order, so grouping them next to `dress` in Python would have left three lists in two orders with nothing comparing them; the cost is that the filter chips read `…Bags, Accessories, Swimwear, Sleepwear` rather than in garment order. **`fit` and `length` apply** because both pass the test the document already uses — a cover-up is `flowy`, a rash guard is `slim`, a nightdress has a hem — and **the three narrowed `fit` words were left alone** because widening them is a claim about garments this wardrobe does not contain yet. **`standalone` layers** because neither is layered in any look this app builds, and a determinate answer keeps `top` the only category in `02` that errors. **The transaction question was measured, not inferred**: on PostgreSQL 18.6 both `ADD VALUE` statements ran inside a `BEGIN`/`COMMIT` with no error, so no `autocommit_block()` is needed — what PostgreSQL still refuses is *using* a value in the transaction that added it, and `0003` uses neither.
**Trade-off accepted:** **`PROMPT_VERSION` moved**, because half the vision prompt is rendered from `enums.py`. That is harmless only because 1.11 is unrun — had the golden-set baseline existed, this task would have invalidated it, which is the concrete reason to land the vocabulary before 1.11 rather than after. **`downgrade()` is a no-op and the two labels survive a downgrade to `0001`.** PostgreSQL has no `DROP VALUE`; the honest reversal is a type swap that rebuilds the column `0001` created and fails outright once any row carries either label. A downgrade that raised instead would have blocked the `alembic downgrade 0001` every mutation run in `06` starts from — so the no-op buys the practice at the price of a type that widens irreversibly. `ADD VALUE IF NOT EXISTS` is what makes the re-upgrade clean. **`bodycon` nulls out on a swimsuit**, which is a real garment word lost on a nullable field. **The two new categories have no photograph anywhere** — not in the seed wardrobe, not in 1.11's golden set — so the vision model's ability to tell a bikini from a bra is untested and will stay that way until someone photographs one. And **the renumber left the dated entries in this file and in `PROGRESS.md` naming the old numbers**: 165 above still says `0003` and `0004` for feedback and trips, and is not rewritten, because it records what was decided on 2026-08-27 before this task ran. The forward-looking references were renumbered; the log was not.

## 168 — `occasion` becomes a closed vocabulary at the task that first accepts it

**Decision:** `02-DATA-MODEL.md` gains an `occasion` section — `casual · work · evening · sport · formal · travel` — and `app/enums.py` an `Occasion(Vocabulary)`. `LookSuggestRequest.occasion` is typed as the enum, so an unknown value is a `422` from Pydantic before a row or a prompt exists. `frontend/src/app/shared/models/enums.ts`, its spec and six `vocabulary.occasion.*` keys in `en.json` land in the same commit. `AUDITS.md` **O-8** closes.
**Alternative:** Type the field `str` and let `04-API-SPEC.md`'s prose remain the only statement of the six, which is what every task before this one did. Add a `CHECK` to `looks.occasion`, or a PostgreSQL `ENUM` type as `category` has. Mirror to `enums.ts` at 2.8 instead, with the chips that render it.
**Reasoning:** O-8 named 2.7 as the place, and the reason is that this is the first task that *accepts* the value: before it, nothing on the wire could carry an occasion, and after it, `looks.occasion` starts filling up. The argument that closed every other list applies unchanged — `work` and `Work` and `office` are the same request — and Stage 3's preference block aggregates over saved looks, which a free-text column makes noisy in a way no later migration can repair retroactively. **No `CHECK` and no PostgreSQL type**, because `02` prints the column as `TEXT` and `trips.occasions` is `JSONB` where a type could not reach at all; the enforcement therefore lives in one place, the request schema, and the document says so rather than implying the database helps. **The mirror lands now** because 2.6a's lesson was that a vocabulary is not one file: it missed `en.json` and would have grown a filter chip reading `vocabulary.category.swimwear`. Mirroring the values is not building the chips, which stay 2.8's.
**Trade-off accepted:** **The database still accepts anything.** A script, a psql session or a future endpoint that forgets the schema can write `"Work"` into `looks.occasion`, and nothing will catch it — this is weaker than `category`, which has a type. It is the honest consequence of `02` printing `TEXT`, and the alternative was inventing a migration this document does not ask for. The frontend mirror also lands one task before anything renders it, which is the same shape as `CONDITIONS` arriving at 2.1 for a strip built at 2.12.

## 169 — The stylist's exclusion list is a setting, and a typo in it fails at boot

**Decision:** `STYLIST_EXCLUDED_CATEGORIES: str = "swimwear,sleepwear"` on `Settings`, comma-separated like `CORS_ORIGINS`, with a `field_validator` refusing any token that is not a `Category` and a derived `stylist_excluded_categories -> frozenset[Category]`. `POST /looks/suggest` filters on it beside `ready` and `is_archived`. `AUDITS.md` **O-21** closes.
**Alternative:** A module constant in `looks.py` or in `enums.py`. A list of `(category, subcategory)` pairs, which is O-21's second option. Coerce unknown names away with a `logger.warning` instead of refusing to start. Filter in `serialize_wardrobe` or in `suggest_looks`.
**Reasoning:** `01-ARCHITECTURE.md:96` and `STAGE-2` 2.4 both promise the list is **configurable**, and this project's configuration mechanism is `Settings` — a constant would keep the filter and break the promise, in a sentence two documents have carried since Stage 0. **Category-only** rather than pairs because 2.6a made both first-class categories precisely so there would be a field to match on; a pair list would be a shape with no second user and a wider surface to get wrong. **A typo fails at process start** because the failure mode is silent: `STYLIST_EXCLUDED_CATEGORIES=swimwear,pyjamas` filters swimwear, filters nothing else, and looks exactly like a working configuration — the same class of invisible failure O-21 spent two tasks making impossible. Boot is also where `JWT_SECRET`'s length is already enforced. **The filter is the route's** because 2.3 and 2.4 both said so in writing: a serialiser that decided membership could not be tested as a format, and `suggest_looks` serialises exactly what it is handed.
**Trade-off accepted:** **A deployment can turn the guard off**, and emptying the variable is a supported thing to do — `DECISIONS.md` 002 argues for the whole wardrobe everywhere except this one sentence, so an operator who agrees with 002 can have it. `Category.not_in(())` compiles to a true predicate, which is why an empty list is legal rather than accidental. And the exclusion is by category, so a swim shorts item mis-tagged as `bottom` still reaches the model — the filter is only as good as the tagging, which is the honest limit of any server-side exclusion.

## 170 — `position` is written from the model's ordering, `role` is left `NULL`

**Decision:** `POST /looks/suggest` writes `look_items.position` from the index of the model's `item_ids` and leaves `role` `NULL`. No `Role` vocabulary is added to `02-DATA-MODEL.md`, `enums.py` or `enums.ts`. `AUDITS.md` **O-25** stays open on the role half with **2.11** named as owner, and `02` says in prose why the section is absent.
**Alternative:** Derive `role` from `Category` through an explicit map, which is O-25's first option and forces an answer about `dress` and about `outerwear`/`outer`. Leave both columns unwritten, which is what 2.6 shipped. Write `role` as the category name verbatim, contradicting `04-API-SPEC.md`'s `replace_role` spelling.
**Reasoning:** The two columns look symmetric and are not. **`position` has one obvious value and no vocabulary question**: the model returns an ordered list, the composite primary key imposes no order of its own, and if nothing writes the index the ordering is destroyed at the moment it is persisted — for a column `02` already prints, defaulted to `0`. **`role` cannot be written without deciding a vocabulary**, and `04`'s six values do not cover `Category`: `dress` maps to none of them, `outerwear` is spelled `outer`, and after 2.6a there are nine categories to seven of which a look can even refer. Deciding that here would be settling 2.11's field on its behalf, one task early, from prose rather than from `02` — which is exactly what 166 refused at 2.6 and what `CLAUDE.md` calls inventing a name. Nothing reads `role` before 2.11, and 2.11 can derive it at read time from the category it already has.
**Trade-off accepted:** **A nullable column stays null for a stage**, which reads as an unfinished write to anyone comparing the table against the DDL, and the answer is in `02` and in O-25 rather than in the row. The asymmetry — one column written, its neighbour not — needs the docstring beside it to say why, which is a comment the code would not otherwise carry. And `position` is written **without a reader**: 2.9 orders the look card by `layer` and `category`, so until 2.11 the column records something nothing consumes. That is accepted because the alternative is not "no cost" but "the information is gone".

## 171 — One retry for a violation, none for silence

**Decision:** `POST /looks/suggest` calls the stylist, judges the answer with `validate_look_response`, and on a violation calls **once** more with `correction=` set to that violation. A second failure is `502 stylist_failed`. A `ValueError` from `suggest_looks` — no choices, no content, unparseable JSON, wrong shape — and any `OpenAIError` are `502 stylist_failed` **without** a retry. Three `logger.warning` paths, one user-facing message.
**Alternative:** Retry every failure, including a timeout and an unparseable body. Retry a `ValueError` with no correction, since the model might do better unprompted. Let the provider's exceptions escape into a `500`. Distinguish the failures on the wire with three codes.
**Reasoning:** The retry exists to carry a sentence back to the model — that is the whole of what `correction=` is (`DECISIONS.md` 162, 164). A failure with no violation to name has nothing to put in it, so a second call is a coin flip that costs the whole wardrobe again and another four to eight seconds on a request the user is already waiting on. `03-AI-CONTRACTS.md`'s failure table says the same thing about the case it happens to name: "OpenAI timeout (> 30s) … the request is not retried automatically". **`OpenAIError` is caught explicitly** because 161 deliberately let it escape `suggest_looks` so that this route could tell "the provider did not answer" from "the provider answered nonsense"; letting it reach FastAPI would be the bare `500` with no `code` that 043 and 161 both exist to prevent. **One code, three logs** because the user reads one sentence either way — `03`'s table gives the timeout and the invalid answer the same words — and a frontend branch that cannot change what it renders is a branch that should not exist.
**Trade-off accepted:** **A transient provider blip costs the user the whole request**, where a blind retry would sometimes have rescued it. That is the documented behaviour rather than a regression, and the mitigation is the "Try again" button `05-FRONTEND-SPEC.md` already puts on the look card. **Two calls maximum means the wardrobe is serialised twice**, which is the token cost 2.3's measurement made affordable (about 4,600 tokens for 150 items). And the three failures are only distinguishable in the log, so a support question about a `502` needs the log line rather than the response body.

## 172 — The response carries the row's `id`, and the guard counts the wardrobe that is sent

**Decision:** `LookSuggestResponse` is `03-AI-CONTRACTS.md`'s answer with `item_ids` replaced by hydrated `items` and each look carrying the `id` of the row just persisted. `is_saved`, `feedback` and `worn_at` stay out. `wardrobe_too_small` is measured over the **filtered** list — `ready`, not archived, not excluded — from the rows already fetched, with no second `COUNT`, and before the forecast call as well as before the model.
**Alternative:** Return the answer without an id and let 3.2 re-fetch `GET /looks` to find the row it was just handed. Count every `ready` row, which is what `04-API-SPEC.md` said in one line. Count with a `SELECT count(*)` before loading the wardrobe. Include `is_saved: false` so the card can render its heart without knowing the default.
**Reasoning:** **The id is the identity of a resource this call created**, not a field the model invented — `look_id` was struck from `STYLIST_SCHEMA` at 2.4 for exactly that distinction. Every other resource in this API answers with its `id`, and without it the one screen that has a look in front of it cannot name it: 3.2's heart button is on this card. **The guard counts what is sent** because that is the only number that means anything after 169 — six garments the stylist never sees cannot make an outfit, and counting them produces a `502` from a wardrobe whose real problem was that it was too small. Counting from the loaded rows rather than a `COUNT` keeps it one query and makes "the number checked" and "the number sent" the same object. **Before the forecast** because `06-TESTING-STRATEGY.md` asks for a test proving no AI call is attempted, and a small wardrobe is knowable without spending anything at all.
