**Superseded at 219 in its rendering, not in its reasoning.** The greeting is still not `userLabel()`, still takes the nameless key on a blank display name rather than dropping a placeholder, and still puts the name alone in the content face. What changed is the face around it — italic Cormorant at 20px — and a full stop at the end of all six strings, because the mockup's line is a sentence.

## 216 — The empty states stop being a dashed box

**Decision:** `EmptyState` loses its dashed border, its `surface-elevated` fill, its centring and its own padding. It becomes a left-aligned group in the content column: the title in `font-display text-3xl`, the description in **`font-prose text-lg`** — the authored prose face's first rendered surface — and the projected CTA below it. **The `icon` input is deleted.** The CTA's weight varies per caller: the wardrobe's first-upload button stays filled, `/saved`'s becomes `ghost`, and the no-match state was already ghost.

**Alternative:** Keep the box and restyle inside it. Keep `icon` and finally pass one at all three call sites. One CTA weight for all three, on the grounds that a shared component should look shared.

**Reasoning:** **A dashed rectangle is the most generic shape in web design, and it was carrying three of the highest-stakes screens in the product** — including the wardrobe's first-upload state, which is the second screen a new account ever sees. The copy inside it is good (*"Photograph your clothes and Bijoux tags them for you — colour, fit, warmth and the rest"*) and it was being set at 14px inside a dotted box. **The `icon` input was built at DR.2 and passed by none of its three call sites**, so the affordance has never rendered; it existed for the box aesthetic this task removes, and a line icon inside a written note is that aesthetic surviving its container. Deleting it costs one spec test and removes the invitation to reintroduce what is being taken out. **No padding of its own**, which is the cleanest expression of "no box": a box has padding and a note does not, the page's `gap-region` already puts air above it (212), and a component that added more would be fighting DR.7 for the same space.

**The CTA's weight varies because the callers do, and the component already said so.** `saved-looks.page.ts` projects its CTA rather than taking it as an input *"because the copy and the way out differ per caller"* — that sentence predates this task and this is it being taken at its word. An empty wardrobe has exactly one thing the user must do and it earns a filled button; an empty `/saved` is a state a working account passes through on the way to asking for a look, and a filled button there competes with the wardrobe's for the same meaning.

**`text-lg` for the body rather than `text-base`**, which is the loading captions' size at DR.11b: an empty state's description is the only prose on a screen with nothing else on it, so it carries hero weight that a caption does not. Both are on Tailwind's scale; neither is an arbitrary `text-[17px]`.

**Trade-off accepted:** **The framing is asserted by nothing.** Three mutations were run and caught with control green at both ends — swapping the prose face for the system stack killed 1, demoting the `h2` killed 3, rendering the description unconditionally killed 1 — but **the absence of a border, the alignment and the CTA weights are carried by this entry and by review, not by a test.** Asserting the absence of a class is brittle enough that it would fail on an unrelated rename, so the box can be re-added by anyone who thinks the empty state looks unfinished. **`--font-prose` now renders, so DR.9's weaker green is closed** — but by one component, on three screens, and the second face is still doing very little work until DR.14. And **the `icon` deletion is not recoverable from the type system**: a future caller wanting one will re-add the input rather than find it, which is the intended outcome and also means the reasoning lives only here.

**Two adjacent things recorded rather than left to be noticed in a third pass.** **`Skeleton` has had exactly one caller since DR.2** — the stylist's wait — which is the same standing `AuthoredLine` has today: a `shared/ui/` component placed ahead of `AUDITS.md` O-15's second-caller rule. DR.11b either gives it two more and clears O-15 outright, or leaves it a conscious placement still waiting. **And the error states are a deliberate deferral, not an omission.** Every error in the application is the same object — `<p class="text-sm font-medium text-danger">` — one treatment for a failed wardrobe load, a wrong password and a failed swap. They are the third member of the empty/loading/error family and share its shape: nothing to show, and writing that says why. The copy is already the best in the product; only the framing is missing. **DR.16**, after the ground.

**Amended at DR.11b — the principle the missing padding was an instance of, and one open question closed.** `EmptyState` has no vertical padding because **a piece of vertical space is owned by one layer of the layout: the container, which can see its neighbours, not the component, which can only see itself.** If both add, they compound. An `EmptyState` given `py-8` "to make it breathe" would claim 72px above and below where every other region on the page claims 40, push its neighbours apart, and — since an empty state is usually the only thing on its screen — unbalance the page against the hero band it sits under. The rhythm would decay without a single test or lint rule noticing, which puts it in the same enforcement class as `gap-3` against `gap-group`: the record and the review are the whole of it. **And `Skeleton` clears `AUDITS.md` O-15 outright at DR.11b**, going from the one caller it had held since DR.2 to three — the stylist's wait, the wardrobe grid and the saved stack. The standing question this entry left open is closed; `AuthoredLine`'s closed at DR.12, when the weather strip became its second caller.

**Superseded at 219, and the only part of the DR pass this commit does not carry out.** `EmptyState` is shared with `/saved`, and 219 converts one screen at a time, so the wardrobe's empty state and its no-match state are the one region of the screen still drawn in the pre-Atelier language: Fraunces title, `appButton` call to action. The commit that converts `/saved` converts this component with it.

## 217 — The loading states, and why the whole region defers rather than the skeleton

**Decision:** The wardrobe and `/saved` draw a skeleton in the shape of what is coming — six tiles in the grid's own columns, two look-card blocks — beside an announced status line. Trip detail keeps prose. All three sit inside `animate-deferred`, a **250ms show-delay** declared once in `tailwind.css`, so a response that beats the delay renders content with no skeleton at all. The wardrobe's caption gains the `role="status"` the other two already had, and all three captions move to `font-prose text-base`.

**Alternative:** Prose everywhere, better set. A skeleton on trip detail as well. A `showSkeleton` signal flipped by `setTimeout` instead of a CSS delay. Filling the viewport with tiles rather than six.

**Reasoning:** **Deferring only the skeleton makes the screen worse, and this is the finding the entry exists for.** The naive version puts the caption on screen at 0ms, the tiles at 250ms and the content at 400ms: **three states where there was one**, a flash traded for a stutter. So the delay wraps the whole loading region — skeleton and status line in one deferred container — and the screen changes once. A later edit meaning to "fade the skeleton in nicely" would reintroduce exactly this, which is why it is written down rather than left in the markup.

**Opacity, never `visibility`, and that is DECISIONS.md 066 read the other way round.** 066 refused opacity for the boot hint because *"an opacity-0 element is still in the accessibility tree, so a screen reader would announce this line immediately."* Here that is precisely the intent: a screen-reader user is told the wardrobe is loading at once, while a sighted user sees nothing until the wait has proved itself real. Same property, opposite purpose, stated so a later reader does not think 066 was forgotten. **There is deliberately no base `opacity: 0`** either — the animation's `both` fill mode supplies the from-state, so an environment that suppresses animations shows the skeleton immediately rather than never.

**CSS rather than a signal and a timer**, on the shape of the two failure modes. An untested CSS delay fails cosmetically — the skeleton appears at once — and is visible the first time anyone opens the screen. A mis-owned timer fails as a callback outliving its component, which is `DECISIONS.md` 107 in this project's own record. `index.html`'s boot hint already runs this exact pattern, so the mechanism has precedent in the tree. **The Tailwind mechanism was verified rather than assumed**, as `--spacing-hero` was: `@keyframes` nested inside `@theme static` does reach the built stylesheet, alongside `.animate-deferred{animation:var(--animate-deferred)}` and the token itself.

**Six tiles and two cards, not a screenful.** A skeleton is a promise about the response, and filling the viewport promises a warehouse to an account with four garments. Six is two rows on a phone and just over one on a desktop — enough to say *grid*. **Trip detail gets no skeleton at all** because its shape depends on the response: how many days, and whether each has a look. A skeleton there would promise a layout the trip may not have, which is worse than the plain caption it replaces.

**Trade-off accepted:** **The delay is asserted by nothing.** jsdom runs no animations, so the 250ms is carried by this entry and by looking at the screen; the tile counts, the status role and the skeletons' presence are tested, and the thing that makes them not flash is not. **The captions still say the same words** — `wardrobe.loading` and its two siblings are untouched here, so a skeleton now sits above a sentence that was written to be the whole of the wait; DR.14 is where that copy is re-read. **No `prefers-reduced-motion` guard**, consistent with the unguarded `animate-pulse` and `animate-spin` already in the tree, which means the project now has three animations and no policy — worth one line in a later task rather than an exception made here. And **a mutation exposed a pre-existing fragility in `saved-looks.page.spec.ts`**: reducing the card count killed 1 test directly and 14 more as collateral, because that file's `afterEach` runs `mock.verify()` before `TestBed.resetTestingModule()`, so any test that fails before flushing its request leaves a dirty TestBed for every test after it. File-wide, older than this task, and not fixed in it.

**Superseded at 219 in its shape, not in its argument.** The whole loading region still defers on `--animate-deferred`, the count is still six, and the status line still carries the wait for a screen reader while the tiles stay `aria-hidden`. What changed is the shape the skeleton promises: four columns of 4:5 plates rather than three squares, because a skeleton is the grid's own shape and the grid changed.

## 218 — The weather strip becomes a sentence, and the vocabulary decides its word order

**Decision:** The strip's two lines become one. `wardrobe.weather.reading` narrows to `"{{temp}}°C"` and keeps the display face; `wardrobe.weather.place` retires; `wardrobe.weather.sentence` — **`"{{condition}} in {{city}} today"`** — is rendered by `AuthoredLine` with the condition in `[params]` and the city in `[content]`, so the city alone leaves the prose face. The degraded "set your home city" line moves to prose with it. The card, the three states and every behaviour are unchanged.

**Alternative:** Keep the two-key split and only restyle it. Word the sentence *"It's clear in {{city}} today"* and add a lowercase condition vocabulary. Remove the card while the file is open.

**Reasoning:** **The two-key split was a workaround, and this entry is where it stops being one.** `weather-strip.ts` has explained since 2.2 that *"the split is what makes the display face legal here"* — the line was cut in half because 071 had no way to put a geocoded name inside an authored sentence, so the only way to keep Fraunces away from the city was to give the city a line of its own. `AuthoredLine` is the answer that split was standing in for; the key is whole again, and a translation may put the city placeholder first with only the city moving.

**The vocabulary's existing shape decided the sentence's word order, and that is the finding worth keeping.** The obvious wording is *"It's clear in Tel Aviv today"* — and `vocabulary.condition.*` is capitalised for chip use, so it renders *"It's **C**lear in Tel Aviv today"*. The fixes were all worse than the problem: lowercasing in code is wrong for languages that capitalise differently, and a second lowercase vocabulary is seventeen more keys and two spellings of one word. **Leading with the condition makes the existing capitalisation correct** — *Clear in…*, *Partly cloudy in…*, *Thunderstorm in…* — for all eight conditions, with no new keys and no lowercasing. The sentence bent around the token rather than the token being duplicated to fit the sentence.

**The card stays**, because DR.15 decides what the ground looks like and judging a card against a ground about to be warmed and grained is one confused decision instead of two clear ones.

**Trade-off accepted:** **The existing tests caught nothing, and finding that out was worth more than the rewrite.** All four survived the two-keys-to-one change untouched, because they asserted the fragments `30°C`, `Clear` and `Tel Aviv` — every one of which is on screen either way. They were pinning the words and not the sentence. One is now strengthened to assert the whole sentence and a fifth test asserts the city, and only the city, in the content face; three mutations were caught with control green at both ends — moving the condition into `[content]`, swapping the two dictionaries, and pointing the component at the wrong key. **A backtick and an interpolation pair nearly shipped inside the template literal**: the comment being rewritten here sits inside the component's own backticked template, so a backtick in it ends the string and braces are read by the parser. The comment now says so, because the next person to edit the most heavily-commented block in the file will not otherwise know. **`wardrobe.weather.place` is deleted rather than deprecated**, so anything holding that key gets the key back as its own text — the documented miss behaviour, findable on screen. And **`AuthoredLine` now has two callers, which closes the O-15 question 216 left open**; `Skeleton` closed its own at DR.11b.

**Superseded at 219.** The sentence survives and so do its two dictionaries; the card around it does not. This entry deferred the card explicitly — *"DR.15 decides what the ground looks like and judging a card against a ground about to be warmed and grained is one confused decision instead of two clear ones"* — and the picked mockup decides both at once. On the cream ground the card is the only raised object on a screen whose subject is photographs, so the strip is one italic line, a mono reading and a rule.

## 219 — The design pass restarts from picked whole-screen mockups, and the wardrobe is the first

**Decision:** DR.7–DR.17 are abandoned as a method. Entries **212, 213, 214, 215, 216, 217 and 218** are marked superseded above, along with 071's DR.8 and DR.9 amendments, and none of them is deleted — the reasoning in each is still the reasoning, and several of the mechanisms survive intact. What is replaced is how the work arrives: instead of a numbered sequence of one-property improvements, a screen is **pitched as three whole mockups, one is picked, and the picked one is implemented in a single commit**. The wardrobe is the first application and the picked direction is **Atelier** — a cream ground, one thin serif, chrome that nearly disappears. **The palette is consolidated onto the token names the whole application already reads**, not held beside them: `--color-canvas` is `#F5F1EA`, `--color-ink` `#2A2320`, `--color-ink-muted` `#7A7358`, `--color-ink-soft` `#B3AC9B`, `--color-line` `#E1DBD1`, `--color-line-strong` `#D0C9B9`, `--color-accent` `#7A7658`. `--color-surface` becomes a warm paper `#FAF7F0` and `--color-surface-elevated` a warm stone `#EAE4D8`; the three accent derivatives are re-struck off the new accent. Type: `--font-display` and `--font-prose` both name **Cormorant Garamond** (300 display, italic prose), `--font-sans` is **Inter** and is the content face, `--font-mono` is **JetBrains Mono** and draws every number on a screen. **So the palette follows through to all nine screens on the day this lands, and nothing else does.** The stylist, saved looks, profile, trips and the auth pair are pitched next, one at a time, and each pitch decides that screen's typography, layout and states.

**Alternative:** Carry on with DR.13–DR.17 as written. Or convert every screen in this commit, since the tokens are global and a half-converted application looks worse than either end state. Or repoint the existing tokens — `--color-canvas` to cream, `--color-ink` to espresso — instead of adding six, which would convert all nine screens from the token file alone and cost nothing at the call sites.

**Reasoning:** **DR.7–DR.12 were rejected as micro-tweaks, and the diagnosis is worth stating precisely because it is not that the decisions were wrong.** Each of the six was defensible on its own — three spacing rungs, an authored-prose face, a segmented line, an undashed empty state, a deferred skeleton, a weather sentence — and every one of them was a *property of a screen* rather than a screen. A design arrived at one property at a time cannot be judged, because at no point is there a thing to judge: the reviewer is asked whether 2.5rem is better than 1.5rem, never whether the wardrobe looks like anything. DR.12's own trade-off records the shape of it — the card was deferred to DR.15 because the ground had not been decided yet, which is a pass admitting that its unit of work is smaller than its unit of judgement. **A whole mockup is the smallest thing that can be picked.** Three of them side by side is the smallest thing that makes picking mean something.

**The tokens are repointed and not prefixed, and this was decided twice.** The first cut of this commit added six `--color-atelier-*` tokens beside the existing thirteen, on the argument that repointing would give eight unpitched screens a design by inheritance. That argument confuses two different things. A **palette is not a design**; it is the substrate a design is drawn on, and it was already global — one `@theme` block under nine screens — so the honest choice was never *one palette or two*, it was *which values the one palette carries*. Holding a second, prefixed set did not protect the other eight screens from anything: it left them on colours the project had already stopped believing in, and it put the cost of the transition on every future call site, which would have had to know which of two names to write. **What the per-screen pitch actually owns is typography, layout, states and copy** — the things that make a screen look like itself — and every one of those is still per-screen after this. The stylist gets cream and olive today; it gets its type, its rhythm and its states when it is pitched.

**The consequence, stated plainly so nobody is surprised by it: eight screens change colour in this commit and none of them was reviewed for it.** That is accepted rather than overlooked. The failure mode it opens is narrow — a token pair that read well on `#FAFAF8` and reads badly on `#F5F1EA` — and it is visible on sight, where the alternative failure mode (two live palettes, indefinitely) is invisible and compounds at every new call site. **Three tokens outside the brief's list were re-struck for exactly this reason.** `--color-accent-hover`, `--color-accent-soft` and `--color-accent-wash` were DR.1's blues; left alone they would have put a cool `#EEF0F2` wash behind olive text in the navigation bar above *every* screen. `--color-surface` moved off white to `#FAF7F0` because §5 of the brief requires the insights panel to stop reading as a white card on cream and forbids redesigning it — a palette-only fix is the only fix left. `--color-surface-elevated` moved to `#EAE4D8`, a step *darker* than the ground, which is the value the brief specified for the skeleton and is right for its other six consumers too: on warm paper a panel is lifted by tint, never by white.

**Google Fonts, against 065's self-hosting.** 065 self-hosted one display family as one subsetted variable file and preloaded it, and the arithmetic held for one face. Atelier is three families with an italic and two weights among them, and the subsetting, the `unicode-range` and the version pinning would be three more hand-maintained artefacts in `public/fonts/` before the direction has survived its second screen. The two `preconnect` hints are what keep the cost to one round trip rather than two. Fraunces stays self-hosted and preloaded because eight screens still set their titles in it; when the last one converts, the `@font-face`, the preload and the woff2 all go together.

**Trade-off accepted:** **Eight screens are recoloured unreviewed**, which is the consolidation's bill and is argued above rather than hidden here. They are also now *half* converted — Atelier's palette under DR.1's typography and layout — which is a real intermediate state and reads as one on `/stylist` and `/trips` until they are pitched. **Three faces now block first paint from a third-party origin**, where 065 had a preload from our own. **Fraunces stays in the repository, preloaded, for one consumer**: the pre-bootstrap marker, which 066 requires to paint before the bundle and which therefore cannot wait on a Google Fonts stylesheet. That is 67 KB preloaded for a word that shows for a moment, and it is the next thing to question. **The accent is `#7A7658` on `#F5F1EA`, which is 4.1:1** — under AA for normal text, and it is what "Style me", "Clear filters" and every link on a converted screen are drawn in. `--color-accent-wash` behind `--color-accent` in the navigation bar is 3.8:1 where DR.1's blues were 8.3:1. The brief put an accessibility rewrite out of scope deliberately; this entry records the number so the rewrite has somewhere to start. **The chip is written out in `filter-bar.ts` instead of coming from the shared `appChip`**, because the directive sets its font size in the base string every variant shares and 11px cannot be reached from a call site — so the wardrobe's chips and five other screens' chips are now two implementations of one idea, with nothing but this entry holding them together. **`AUDITS.md` O-15 is answered a third time and stays open.** **And "pieces" is the wardrobe's word in seven keys and "items" in seven others.** Moved: the four `wardrobe.count.*`, the two `wardrobe.insights.neverWorn.*`, and `wardrobe.upload.open`. Not moved: `wardrobe.tagging.one`/`.other`, `wardrobe.empty.cta`, `wardrobe.upload.title`, `wardrobe.upload.tip`, `wardrobe.item.untagged` and `wardrobe.filter.noMatch.body`. The screen no longer contradicts itself in the two sentences that sit one line apart, which was the reported fault, and it is not yet of one vocabulary.

**Recorded as one line, because it is the sentence that will be quoted:** the old `canvas`/`ink`/`ink-muted`/`ink-soft`/`line`/`line-strong`/`accent`/`surface` tokens now carry Atelier's values, the `atelier-` prefix and `--font-atelier-display` are deleted, and `--font-display` names Cormorant Garamond. **The palette follows through to every screen; per-screen typography and layout land in each screen's own whole-screen pitch.**

## 220 — The Ritual: the form stops being a step, and the look stops being a card

**Decision:** The stylist is pitched as three whole mockups and **Direction 3, The Ritual**, is picked and built. One behaviour change carries the whole direction: **the form never leaves the screen.** `@if (isSuggesting) / @else if (look) / @else (form)` becomes a header, a permanent form, and one slot beneath it holding a skeleton, the look, or a muted line reading *"Ready when you are."* Consequences, all of them deliberate: the submit button's label comes from the page as a key — `stylist.submit` with no look, `stylist.submit.restyle` with one — so the parent decides and the form only prints it; **a field changed under a look does not invalidate the look**, which stays until the button is pressed; and *Try again* clears the look rather than re-mounting anything. The forecast moves out of the form into the header beside the `h1`. **`LookCard` loses its wrapper** — no card, no fill, no shadow — and becomes a title row with a mono piece count, a message, a four-column strip, one reasoning line, a weather note and a row of circular controls, sitting directly on the canvas. **Layer grouping is deleted**: the sort by layer then category survives, the `<h3>` headings and the `groups` computed do not, and the layer prints under each tile as `Base layer · Tops`. `ItemCard` gains a `caption` input, defaulted on, so the strip can turn the wardrobe's colour caption off and write its own.

**Alternative:** Direction 1, *The Letter* — the form as a filled sentence, the result as a stylist's typed note, prose leading and photographs beneath. Direction 2, *The Spread* — an editorial two-column layout with the reasoning in a sidebar. Within Direction 3: keep the branch chain and only restyle it, which is every visual change here with none of the behaviour; or recalculate the look automatically when a field changes, which the pitch's own caption proposes.

**Reasoning:** **The Ritual was picked for what it is like on the twentieth visit rather than the first.** The Letter is the best of the three to demonstrate once and the worst to use daily: every reroll is a full-page state change, and the prose that makes it impressive is the part a returning user skips. The Spread is a layout for a result, and this screen is three-quarters of the time not showing one. The Ritual is the only one of the three whose empty state and result state are **the same screen with something added**, which is what makes the form a control rather than a step.

**The look is not invalidated by an edit, and that is the decision inside the decision.** The pitch's caption says "change any field and the result recalculates in place", and that was declined: it means a request per keystroke in the notes field, a look that changes while being read, and no way to compare what you have against what you would get. **Opt-in re-request keeps the screen honest** — what is on screen is the answer to the question that was actually asked, and the button says which of the two things it will do. That is also why the label lives on the page: only the page knows whether a look exists, and a form that worked it out for itself would need to be told about the look, which is the input the arrangement exists to avoid.

**The card wrapper went because there was nothing to separate the look from.** A card earns its edges when it sits among peers — `/saved` is a stack of them and keeps its own counsel — but this look is the last thing on the page and the only object below the form, so the wrapper was a box drawn around the sole occupant of the room. **The layer headings went for the same reason at a smaller scale**: four caps headings over four garments is more chrome than the thing it organises, and the fact each heading carried — which layer this piece is — is one line under the tile it belongs to now, where it sits beside the category rather than replacing it.

**071 wins over the mockup three times, and it is worth naming where.** The picked direction draws the look title, the message, the reasoning and every garment name in italic Cormorant. All four are **the model's prose or the model's names**, which 071 defines as content, and the wardrobe tile caption was reverted from exactly this face one commit ago for exactly this reason — Cormorant Garamond is latin-subset, so a Hebrew garment name set in it falls back per character. They are built in the content face **at the direction's sizes**: the title is 28px, the reasoning 16px and italic, the message and the note italic beneath them. That is 071 read as it is written — *what content gains in a redesign is size and leading, never a face* — and the direction survives it, because what makes the Ritual the Ritual is the compactness and the persistence, not the serif. The authored faces stay where the words are ours: the `h1`, the piece count, the tile meta lines, the missing-pieces label, the waiting line and the *Ready when you are.*

**The look card is self-describing, and the snapshot is the reason it can be.** The form staying editable under a result buys the direction its whole feel and costs it one thing: a look on screen stops saying what it is a look *for*. So the card heads with a mono kicker — `CASUAL · TUE 1 SEPT · AUTO COAT` — built from the draft **as it was when the request went out**, held in a page signal called `answered`, set in `suggest()` and cleared in `startOver()`. A card reading `draft()` live would relabel itself the moment a chip was tapped and quietly claim the look answered a question nobody asked it. **The kicker and the form disagreeing is the feature**: it is how a reader tells a look built for Tuesday from a form now set to Wednesday, and it is why *Change & restyle* is a button rather than an effect. A swap leaves the snapshot alone — same request, one role replaced. **Notes are excluded** because they are free text that can run to a paragraph, and what is on the line is what the request *narrowed*; the notes are what widened it.

**Trade-off accepted:** **The screen reads less like the mockup than the wardrobe did**, and the gap is the face on four runs of text. If the intent was the serif rather than the rule, the change is four class swaps in `look-card.ts` and an amendment to 071 — but it should be that amendment and not a quiet exception. **The chip constants are duplicated for the second time.** `look-request-form.ts` now carries the same `CHIP` and `CHIP_STATES` as `filter-bar.ts`, because the shared `appChip` directive still sets its font size in the base string every variant shares. Two identical declarations is a coincidence; a third would be a pattern, and the third screen to need it should convert the directive instead. **The action buttons are 44px, not the mockup's 40**, which is the same departure the wardrobe's chips made from the same floor. **`ItemCard` grew an input to serve a screen it is not on**, which is the shape of dependency the badge was deliberately kept out of that component to avoid — a boolean is cheaper than the alternatives (a second tile component, or a look card that re-implements the photograph, its link and its alt text), and it is the thing to watch if a third caller wants a fourth caption. **Ten i18n keys were needed where the brief predicted two.** New: `stylist.submit.restyle`, `stylist.ready`, `stylist.weather.reading`, `stylist.look.meta`, `stylist.look.pieces.one`, `stylist.look.pieces.other`, `stylist.look.answered` and the three `stylist.look.coat.*`. Retired: `stylist.today`, `stylist.weather`, `stylist.look.layerOther` and `stylist.look.whyThis`. Changed: `stylist.title`, which read *"Style me"* and now reads *"Stylist"* — it was the same string as the submit button, which is only defensible while the two are never on screen together, and after this they always are. **The kicker brings 211's formatter back four paragraphs after 220 retired it**, which is recorded at 211 rather than hidden: nineteen English month and weekday names still reach the screen from outside `en.json`, and `en-GB` abbreviates September to *Sept*, so the line reads `TUE 1 SEPT` where the pitch drew `TUE 1 SEP` — the locale's answer, not a typo, and trimming a localised string to three characters is how that kind of fix breaks in the next locale. **Two comments elsewhere went stale and were corrected**: `saved-looks.page.ts` and `trip-look.ts` both refused to reuse `LookCard` partly because it *"groups by layer"*, which stopped being true in this commit; both conclusions survive on their other grounds, and both sentences now say so. And **the header forecast is two elements rather than one sentence**: the condition is a single word from a closed vocabulary, so there is no sentence to fragment and 218's objection does not reach it, but the line is now assembled in the template and a translator cannot reorder the dot.
